Document 167: 8月国内宏观经济分化格局延续，美联储如期降息25bp叠加油价回落长债收益率回落：债市基本面和资金面缺乏共振，维持债市中期震荡消化前期涨幅判断

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月国内宏观经济分化格局延续，美联储如期降息25bp叠加油价回落长债收益率回落：债市基本面和资金面缺乏共振，维持债市中期震荡消化前期涨幅判断.pdf

### Page 3

逻辑与观点：债市基本面和资金面缺乏共振，维持债市中期震荡消化前期涨幅判断
◆ 资金面与政策面：资金面，15—17日税期走款集中落地，叠加政府债缴款等资金需求，DR001、DR007周中一度升至1.47%和1.45%左右。随着税期影响消退，周五DR001回落至1.4277%，DR007回落至1.4396%。全
周来看，DR001较上周上行约7BP，DR007上行约1.6BP，资金价格中枢较月初有所抬升 。买断式逆回购：6个月期等量续作，9月两品种均无加量。9月15日，央行以固定数量、利率招标、多重价位中标方式开展
5000亿元6个月期买断式逆回购操作 ，对冲当月5000亿元到期，实现等量续作。结合9月7日3个月期买断式逆回购同样等量续作 ，9月两个期限品种合计等量续作 ，结束了此前7月、8月连续两个月的加量续做。
经济走弱央行降息动作非常谨慎为何？核心在于当前银行息差处于1.4%水平，继续压缩不符合宏观审慎的货币政策意图，因此降息动作会显得非常困难，这也是债券虽然有方向但是缺乏弹性的核心原因。另外

从定价上来看，当前国内CPI水平大致位于1%略低位置，从政府角度上看有推动通胀回升的诉求 ，也就是说CPI通胀继续回落的概率不大 ，即便是回落也很难大幅低于1%。当前国有大行和全国股份制银行的1年
存款利率处于1%或略高一点水平。因此进一步压降储蓄端存款利率难度也较大。因此债券运行基本情景是交易端资金定价的波段行情 。往前看，我们认为债券受益于基本面偏弱环境，整体保持震荡偏强走势，
如果未来能够看到降息动作，反而是波段止盈比较好的位置。
◆ 基本面：国内方面，当周央行与国家统计局先后发布8月金融与经济数据，金融端，社融和贷款增速继续下降 ，由于地产持续收缩推动了直接融资占比历史性超过贷款 ，标志着中国融资结构正在发生变化 。住
户贷款净减少1.03万亿元，居民去杠杆与企业加杠杆并存 ，信贷资源配置的效率正在提升 ，但居民端的疲弱仍是制约信用扩张的关键短板 。实体端，工业生产以5.2%的增速超预期回升，高技术制造业16.7%的
增速和超过六成的新动能贡献率 ，表明新质生产力正在从概念走向实绩 。但消费增速放缓至0.4%、投资降幅扩大至-7.2%，内需修复的节奏显著滞后于生产端 。金融总量宽松与实体数据偏弱之间的 “温差”，

指向宽货币到宽信用的传导仍需疏通 。7月30日政治局会议“宏观政策要发力提效”的定调，与当前“融资结构转型加速、新动能投资逆势加速、但传统内需仍在探底”的数据特征高度呼应——下一步政策重
心预计将继续围绕扩大内需、优化供给展开，货币与财政政策的协同发力仍是关键。海外方面，美联储宣布加息25bps，使基准基金利率从3.50%至3.75%上调到3.75%至4%。美联储在政策声明中指出，由于顽固
的通胀压力以及地缘政治冲突导致的全球能源价格持续走高 ，当前的货币政策在压制通胀回落至2%的目标上仍显不足。美联储当日发布的经济预测摘要显示，美联储官员小幅上调对今年通胀和经济增长的预期，
对今年失业率预测的中值则从6月的4.3%降至4.1%。此外，美联储点阵图显示，美联储18位官员中有12位预计今年还将加息25个基点。美国8月零售销售环比公布为1.2%，高于市场预期，较前值季节偏强上升。
除油品价格上涨带来的影响外，杂货商店与线上零售同比增速均较前值上升，成为本月零售销售增长的主要支撑项。
◆ 观点与策略：9月FOMC会议预期加息，点阵图按照年内再加息。央行与国家统计局先后发布8月金融与经济数据，社融和贷款增速继续下降，由于地产持续收缩推动了直接融资占比历史性超过贷款；住户贷款净

减少1.03万亿元，居民去杠杆与企业加杠杆并存，信贷资源配置的效率正在提升，但居民端的疲弱仍是制约信用扩张的关键短板。实体端，工业生产以5.2%的增速超预期回升，消费增速放缓至0.4%、投资降幅
扩大至-7.2%，内需修复的节奏显著滞后于生产端 。下一步政策重心预计将继续围绕扩大内需 、优化供给展开，货币与财政政策的协同发力仍是关键 。债市或震荡保持震荡，上下均缺乏空间，观望为主。风险
因素：市场博弈复杂度超预期。
返回目录
数据来源：IFinD，中泰期货研究所

### Page 4

上期逻辑与观点：国内资金整体平衡，债券或继续震荡消化前期涨幅
◆ 上期观点与策略：国内国外通胀整体呈上升走势，通胀数据叠加地缘市场定价美联储9月FOMC会议或加息25bp，国内资金整体平衡，债券或继续震荡消化前期涨幅。受到中东局势再次紧张影响，布伦
特原油主力合约价格突破100美金/桶，中国通胀数据回升，美国通胀整体未超预期但核心CPI环比略高于预期，美债收益率大幅上行，根据FED WATCHA数据，截至9月11日收盘，市场定价9月份美联储
会议加息25bp的概率上升至86.6%，年内再加息一次的概率上升至76.6%。受此影响，全球债市和股市表现整体偏弱，能源类商品走势压制力明显。往前看，本次美联储会议加息25bp概率较大，但市
场已经进行了较大程度的定价，因此加息与否的对资产的影响可能已经部分释放，市场可能会更关注点阵图和声明措辞。若点阵图暗示年内还有更多加息（如年内累计加息50bp以上），市场可能进
一步定价10月加息，美元和美债收益率将继续上行；若声明措辞相对克制，市场可能出现“利空出尽”式的反弹。债市方面，经济走弱央行降息动作非常谨慎为何？核心在于当前银行息差处于1.4%

水平，继续压缩不符合宏观审慎的货币政策意图，因此降息动作会显得非常困难，这也是债券虽然有方向但是缺乏弹性的核心原因。另外从定价上来看，当前国内CPI水平大致位于1%略低位置，从政
府角度上看有推动通胀回升的诉求，也就是说CPI通胀继续回落的概率不大，即便是回落也很难大幅低于1%。当前国有大行和全国股份制银行的1年存款利率处于1%或略高一点水平。因此进一步压降
储蓄端存款利率难度也较大。债市或震荡保持震荡，上下均缺乏空间，观望为主。风险因素：市场博弈复杂度超预期。
返回目录
数据来源：IFinD，中泰期货研究所

### Page 7

近期主要宏观经济数据26-9-W3
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，当周央行与国家统计局先后发布8月金融与经济数据，金融端，社融和贷款增速继续下降，由于地产持续收缩推动了直接融资占比历史性超过贷款 ，标志着中国融资结构正在发生变化。
住户贷款净减少1.03万亿元，居民去杠杆与企业加杠杆并存，信贷资源配置的效率正在提升，但居民端的疲弱仍是制约信用扩张的关键短板。实体端，工业生产以5.2%的增速超预期回升，高技术
制造业16.7%的增速和超过六成的新动能贡献率，表明新质生产力正在从概念走向实绩。但消费增速放缓至0.4%、投资降幅扩大至-7.2%，内需修复的节奏显著滞后于生产端。金融总量宽松与实体
数据偏弱之间的“温差”，指向宽货币到宽信用的传导仍需疏通。7月30日政治局会议“宏观政策要发力提效”的定调，与当前“融资结构转型加速、新动能投资逆势加速、但传统内需仍在探底”
的数据特征高度呼应——下一步政策重心预计将继续围绕扩大内需、优化供给展开，货币与财政政策的协同发力仍是关键。

◆ 海外方面，美联储宣布加息25bps，使基准基金利率从3.50%至3.75%上调到3.75%至4%。美联储在政策声明中指出，由于顽固的通胀压力以及地缘政治冲突导致的全球能源价格持续走高，当前的货
币政策在压制通胀回落至2%的目标上仍显不足。美联储当日发布的经济预测摘要显示，美联储官员小幅上调对今年通胀和经济增长的预期，对今年失业率预测的中值则从6月的4.3%降至4.1%。此
外，美联储点阵图显示，美联储18位官员中有12位预计今年还将加息25个基点。美国8月零售销售环比公布为1.2%，高于市场预期，较前值季节偏强上升。除油品价格上涨带来的影响外，杂货商
店与线上零售同比增速均较前值上升，成为本月零售销售增长的主要支撑项。美国7月商业库存环比公布为0.8%，高于预期较前值超季节性上升。本月商业库存环比上升主要由批发商非耐用品库
存，尤其是石油制品库存得超季节性上升拉动。同时，零售业与制造业库存环比均较前值季节性上升。美国8月工业产出环比公布为0%，低于预期较前值季节性下跌，主要由制造业产出环比增速

季节偏强下跌拉动。制造业本月产出的下滑主要由有色金属与化学产品的产出下降拉动。

### Page 9

近期主要宏观经济数据26-9-W2
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，8月外贸保持高增速显韧性，通胀数据整体回升。两大数据共同指向一个核心信号，外需韧性延续、内需价格温和修复，但结构分化的矛盾依然突出。支撑出口的三大逻辑：一是AI投资
支撑全球制造业高景气；二是出口价格增速反弹，且由上游延伸至中下游；三是新能源产业链出口优势持续显现。国家和地区方面，对主要经济体出口均保持正增长：对美出口涨幅扩大，对欧盟出
口涨幅收窄，均受基数因素扰动；受半导体产业链带动，对亚洲出口涨幅扩大。进口方面，自发达经济体进口增速扩大，自新兴市场进口涨幅收窄。品种方面，机电产品继续领涨出口，AI商品出口
的贡献度上升，汽车、船舶增速有所回落；能源矿产出口保持高增长；劳动密集型产品出口增速上升 ，主要由价格支撑。通胀方面，CPI回升的三重驱动力：第一，能源价格反弹是最大拉动因素。
第二，消费电子价格持续上行。 受算力需求快速增长等因素影响，移动电话机、平板电脑、数据存储设备价格环比分别上涨2.3%、2.1%和2.1%。同比维度更为显著：平板电脑上涨21.5%、计算机上

涨19.6%、移动电话机上涨11.0%，涨幅均有扩大。第三，食品价格季节性上涨。PPI回升的核心驱动： 国际原油及有色金属价格上行带动国内相关行业价格上涨。石油开采、精炼石油产品制造、有
机化学原料制造价格分别上涨10.4%、4.1%和0.9%，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格上涨0.8%，4个行业合计影响PPI环比上涨约0.31个百分点，占总环比涨幅的近八成。
◆ 海外方面，通胀粘性强化加息预期。9月10日公布的8月PPI同比上涨5.4%，高于分析师预期的5.3%；环比上涨0.4%，PPI数据公布后，美国10年期国债收益率升至4.88%，反映出市场对通胀持续性的
担忧。美国劳工统计局9月11日公布的数据显示，8月CPI同比上涨3.4%，环比上涨0.4%，主要受能源成本上升推动。核心CPI（剔除食品和能源）同比上涨2.4%，符合预期，并创2021年3月以来最低
水平；核心CPI环比上涨0.3%，略高于市场预期。在油价高企、关税及数据中心投资持续等因素影响下，通胀仍具粘性，数据公布后市场预计美联储在9月15—16日会议上加息的概率约为90%。鉴于

核心CPI环比0.3%超预期、非农就业16.2万远超预期、以及沃什明确的鹰派立场，加息25个基点已是大概率事件。关键变量在于点阵图和声明措辞。若点阵图暗示年内还有更多加息（如年内累计加
息50bp以上），市场可能进一步定价10月加息，美元和美债收益率将继续上行；若声明措辞相对克制，市场可能出现"利空出尽"式的短暂反弹。

### Page 10

近期主要宏观经济数据26-9-W1
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，国家统计局8月31日发布数据显示，8月制造业PMI为49.8%，较7月的49.2%回升0.6个百分点，连续第二个月处于收缩区间。非制造业商务活动指数为49.0%，与7月持平，连续第三个月
处于收缩区间。综合PMI产出指数为49.5%，较7月的49.3%略微回升0.2个百分点。高技术制造业PMI为52.9%，装备制造业为51.4%，继续保持扩张。8月供需同步出现一定回升，新订单反弹幅度最
大，内需边际修复，高端制造持续景气。8月中国宏观数据呈现“低位企稳、结构分化”的特征。官方制造业PMI虽仍处收缩区间，但边际改善明显；财新口径的制造业和服务业PMI均重回扩张，
显示中小企业与服务业景气有所修复。但非制造业商务活动指数连续三个月低于50%，消费整体偏弱，经济内生修复动能仍不稳固
◆ 海外方面，美国劳工部9月4日公布的数据显示，8月非农就业人口增加16.2万人，远超市场预期的5.6万人。7月数据从减少2.3万人上修至增加2.1万人。失业率维持在4.1%，符合预期。U6更广泛

失业率降至7.7%，为2025年6月以来最低 。平均时薪环比上涨0.3%（前值0.2%），同比上涨3.0%（前值3.2%）。就业市场呈现出 “总量强劲 、薪资温和放缓 ”的组合。从结构来看 ，休闲酒店
（+6.2万，其中餐饮+5.9万）、政府（+3.5万）、医疗保健和社会救助（+2.8万）、建筑（+2.2万）与制造（+1.6万）为主要贡献项；信息业（-2.3万）与金融活动（-1.1万）为主要拖累项，零
售（+0.1万）与运输仓储（+0.5万）接近走平。8月ISM PMI：制造业回落，服务业加速扩张。美联储9月加息概率在非农数据公布后显著回升。欧盟统计局9月1日公布的数据显示，欧元区8月CPI
同比上涨3.3%，高于7月的2.9%和市场预期的3.0%。核心CPI同比回落至2.4%（前值2.5%）。能源价格是通胀反弹的主要推手——能源价格同比涨幅较前月扩大0.4个百分点至3.3%。通胀反弹叠加
经济仍具韧性，使欧洲央行面临进一步加息的压力。7月欧元区PPI环比上涨1.6%（前值-0.3%），同比升至5.8%（前值4.6%），能源是主要推手。7月零售销售同比仅增长0.6%，较前值1.4%大幅放

缓。就美联储货币政策方面，8月就业数据客观上提高了年内加息概率，但加息是不是落在9月仍无法定论。我们倾向于9月份出于政治压力考虑+前期没有预期引导，美联储或不加息，如此美联储
滞后于非美央行加息动作，美元指数可能走弱。往前看美国8月CPI大概率是上升的，那么市场预期可能还会扰动，但看到12月份我们倾向于美联储或启动加息。

### Page 11

近期主要宏观经济数据26-8-W4
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，当周发布7月份规上工业企业数据，同比增速回落至17.6%，结构方面AI是绝对主角。1—7月，电子行业利润同比增长1.1倍，拉动全部规上工业企业利润增长9.3个百分点，是利润增长
的核心引擎。其中，集成电路行业利润同比增长18.5倍，对全部电子行业利润增长贡献率超过八成。计算机整机制造、计算机外围设备制造、工业控制计算机及系统制造行业利润分别增长3.3倍、
2.5倍、1.6倍。有色金属冶炼和压延加工业利润增长91.8%，化学原料和化学制品制造业增长56.6%，煤炭开采和洗选业增长50.4%。原材料制造业利润同比增长55.2%。剔除AI和有色行业后，1—7
月工业企业利润同比增长仅为-7.3%。AI（电子行业）和有色两大板块贡献了绝大部分利润增量，其他行业整体利润仍在收缩。
◆ 海外方面，整场演讲释放出鲜明的鹰派信号，核心可概括为：通胀目标坚定不移，金融条件并不紧缩，通胀不降则有“工作要做”；同时彻底放弃前瞻指引，推动美联储进入“更安静”的时代。

在通胀表述方面，强调了：过去65个月持续偏高的通胀，责任理应由央行承担；美国经济和劳动力市场依然具有韧性，当前金融环境很难称得上具有明显限制性。利率工具方面：指出短期利率是
实现双重使命的主要工具。非常规政策手段应仅用于应对真正的危机，在其他情况下应当谨慎使用。经济评估方面，认为劳动力市场稳定经济展现韧性。加息预期骤升，CME 9月加息概率从35%升
至55%—60%，全球主要资产下跌为主，其中贵金属反应较为剧烈，白银下跌接近6个点。

### Page 12

近期主要宏观经济数据26-8-W3
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，国家统计局发布国内宏观经济数据，主要经济指标继续回落，7月GDP增速或处于4.5%以下位置。数据整体延续了生产平稳、内需偏弱、动能向新的格局，工业和服务业生产保持韧性，
但消费与投资增速低位回落，新旧动能的分化进一步加剧。国家统计局新闻发言人付凌晖指出，7月装备制造业、高技术制造业增速分别比上月加快1.3和2.8个百分点，表明制造业正在向产业链、
价值链的中高端转型。国务院全体会议部署经济工作，强调宏观政策要发力提效。往前看宏观政策有望进一步发力对冲经济压力。
◆ 海外方面，美国和欧元区体均呈现服务业扩张加速、制造业增长放缓的分化格局，但欧洲制造业的强劲势头与美国制造业的放缓形成了鲜明对比。服务业是本周美国数据最超预期的亮点。美国服
务业PMI初值从7月的54.6大幅跃升至56.8，不仅明显高于市场预期的54.0，更创下2024年12月以来最高水平。服务业扩张抵消了制造业放缓的影响。制造业是本周欧元区数据的最大亮点。制造业

PMI从7月的51.9升至52.8，创逾四年新高，远超预期的51.8。新订单带动制造业结束连续三年裁员，采购活动小幅上行；投入成本涨幅回落至中东冲突爆发以来最低，产出价格涨势同步收窄。标
普全球经济学家指出，制造业是本轮欧元区经济最大亮点——预防性补库、AI相关产品需求、国防支出利好生产。服务业PMI在7月反弹后保持稳定。美国7月工业产出环比增长0.2%，低于市场预
期的0.3%，但前值从0.1%上修至0.3%。这是工业产出连续第二个月增长。同比增速维持在1.1%，与上月持平。连续两个月增长确认了工业部门的扩张势头 ，但0.2%的增速低于预期，且较6月（修
正后）有所放缓，表明工业复苏的斜率正在趋平。

### Page 13

近期主要宏观经济数据26-8-W2
数据来源：华尔街见闻、金十数据，WIND，CME，中泰期货研究所
◆ 国内方面，7月末社会融资规模存量为463.27万亿元，同比增长7.4%,止跌走平。从结构看，各分项中，实体信贷同比少增，政府债融资、企业债融资、未贴现银行承兑汇票同比多增。信贷方面，
居民短贷和长贷合计减少4602亿元，企业短贷同比改善少减了3000亿元；企业长贷减少2300亿元。政府债券融资增加1.3万亿元，同比增加694亿元，主要是一般国债到期量下降带来的同比改善，
新增专项债与特别国债发行规模环比与同比均在减少。总体来看，信贷是主要拖累项反应社会融资偏弱，需要货币政策加码。
◆ 海外方面，首先，美国7月CPI同比公布为3.4%，符合预期，较前值小幅下降；环比公布为0.1%，符合预期，较前值季节偏强上升。核心CPI同比公布为2.5%，符合预期，较前值小幅下降；环比公
布为0.2%，符合预期，较上月季节性上升。本月CPI同比增速的下降主要由于能源价格的下跌，并由此带动了交通运输等分项的通胀水平下降。其次，美国7月实际收入环比公布为0%，前值由0.8%

下修至0.7%，显示消费者的实际购买力没有得到提升。此外，美国7月最终需求PPI同比公布为4.7%，低于预期与前值；环比公布为0%，低于预期，较前值小幅季节性上升。核心PPI同比为4.2%，
符合预期，较前值下降。本月PPI同比增速的下降主要由于能源价格下降，并由此带动了个人核心消费与服务价格的普遍下降。零售方面，美国7月零售销售环比公布为-0.6%，明显低于预期，较
前值季节性下跌。同比公布为5%，较前值明显下降。本月零售销售的下降主要由汽车及零部件销售额的季节性下降带动 ，家具、电子设备等耐用品销售额同比也出现了下跌。美国8月密歇根大学
消费者信心指数公布为51，未达预期，较前值季节性下跌，位于历史低位。

### Page 14

公开市场操作与货币市场-1
数据来源：IFinD，中泰期货研究所
◆ 资金面，15—17日税期走款集中落地 ，叠加政府债缴款等资金需求 ，DR001、DR007周中一度升至1.47%和1.45%
左右。随着税期影响消退 ，周五DR001回落至1.4277%，DR007回落至1.4396%。全周来看 ，DR001较上周上行约
7BP，DR007上行约1.6BP，资金价格中枢较月初有所抬升。买断式逆回购：6个月期等量续作，9月两品种均无加
量。9月15日，央行以固定数量 、利率招标、多重价位中标方式开展5000亿元6个月期买断式逆回购操作 ，对冲
当月5000亿元到期，实现等量续作 。结合9月7日3个月期买断式逆回购同样等量续作 ，9月两个期限品种合计等
量续作，结束了此前7月、8月连续两个月的加量续做。
◆ 经济走弱央行降息动作非常谨慎为何 ？核心在于当前银行息差处于1.4%水平，继续压缩不符合宏观审慎的货币
政策意图，因此降息动作会显得非常困难 ，这也是债券虽然有方向但是缺乏弹性的核心原因 。另外从定价上来

看，当前国内CPI水平大致位于1%略低位置，从政府角度上看有推动通胀回升的诉求 ，也就是说CPI通胀继续回
落的概率不大，即便是回落也很难大幅低于1%。当前国有大行和全国股份制银行的1年存款利率处于1%或略高一
点水平。因此进一步压降储蓄端存款利率难度也较大 。因此债券运行基本情景是交易端资金定价的波段行情 。
往前看，我们认为债券受益于基本面偏弱环境 ，整体保持震荡偏强走势 ，如果未来能够看到降息动作 ，反而是
波段止盈比较好的位置。

Document 218: Belgium’s economy faces a difficult autumn

Source PDF: all report pdf/Belgium’s economy faces a difficult autumn.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 24 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  24 September 2026   BELGIUM
Belgium’s economy faces a difficult
autumn
For now, the Belgian economy is not heading into recession, but its
resilience is being tested. Renewed energy pressures are likely to push
inflation up to near 5%, while a difficult €10bn budget package will weigh
on demand. Growth should remain weak, leaving little room for policy
errors
We don't expect Belgium to go into recession, but we see growth slowing, inflation near 5% and difficult

choices to be made in terms of public finances
Belgium’s economy is bending, but not breaking – for now
After a modest first quarter, when the economy grew by 0.2% quarter-on-quarter, Belgian GDP
stagnated in the second quarter. On the demand side, household consumption once again
proved resilient, rising by 0.4% despite the first wave of higher energy prices. By contrast,
business investment fell by 0.7%, while public investment dropped by 8.0%. On the supply side,

industry again underperformed, with activity contracting by 0.6% from the previous quarter. In
practical terms, Belgian industrial added value in 2026 remains below its level at the end of
2019.
At the end of June, there were grounds to hope that the easing in energy prices would prove
lasting, paving the way for activity and inflation to normalise. That optimism helped lift both

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 2
consumer and business confidence over the summer. Based on July and August data, the
National Bank of Belgium estimated that growth could then rebound by 0.3% in the third
quarter.
We are less optimistic, however. The escalation in geopolitical tensions since August has
dashed hopes of a swift end to the conflict. In September, diesel prices moved above their
early-April peak. European natural gas prices, meanwhile, are now more than twice their level

at the start of the year, adding to household energy bills. Combined with the first effects of
fiscal tightening, it is hardly surprising that consumer confidence fell back in September to its
May low.
Business confidence improved in September, but the headline indicator owes much to
manufacturing. Confidence in the sector continued to recover, with the index rising from -17.8
in August to -12.2 in September, close to its level before the conflict in the Middle East. Similar,

and somewhat surprising, resilience can be seen elsewhere in Europe. Yet sentiment weakened
markedly in every other sector, with the services' indicator falling to its lowest level since
October 2023. The bottom line is that Belgium’s economy is likely to end the year on a
subdued note. We do not expect a recession, but growth is set to remain very weak.
Inflation likely to hit 5%
With fuel and gas prices rising, inflation moved back above 4% in August. Household gas bills

and motor fuel prices were both more than 24% higher than a year earlier. But petroleum-
related products are not the only source of pressure: household healthcare costs rose by 6.6%,
while recreation and culture prices increased by more than 8%.
In the coming months, inflation is likely to climb further. First, energy prices are still rising,
whereas they were falling a year ago. The annual comparison will therefore become
increasingly unfavourable, mechanically pushing inflation higher. Second, energy prices have

now remained elevated for more than six months, increasing the risk that the shock feeds into
a wider range of consumer prices. Of the 215 goods and services tracked in the index, 46%
recorded a higher inflation rate in August than in July. This is still well below the peak seen in
spring 2022, when inflation accelerated for almost three-quarters of the measured items. Even
so, the energy shock appears to be spreading more broadly through the economy.

The Federal Planning Bureau recently indicated that inflation could temporarily exceed 5%
from the end of this year. Unless geopolitical conditions improve decisively, our own forecasts
point in the same direction.
A moment of truth for Belgium’s public finances
The next few weeks will be decisive for Belgium’s public finances. The prime minister is seeking
an agreement on €10bn of recurring savings in the federal budget. The exercise will not be

### Page 3

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 September 2026 3
straightforward. From an accounting perspective, raising revenue or cutting expenditure is
relatively simple. Designing measures that limit the damage to household purchasing power
and economic activity is much harder – and politically, it may prove close to impossible.
Further tensions among the federal coalition parties therefore look likely. The worst-case
outcome – not our base case, but a genuine tail risk – would be the collapse of the

government. That would undermine household and business confidence, hurting growth, while
investors would probably demand a higher risk premium on Belgian government debt. Even if,
as we expect, the federal government reaches an agreement, the economy is unlikely to
rebound immediately. Bringing public finances under greater control could eventually support
consumption and investment, but in the near term fiscal tightening will inevitably weigh on

Document 226: Capital Market Outlook (2026-09-14)

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

through 2026; 2026 reflects the first quarter.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we
1 Source: Bloomberg as of September 8, 2026.
2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic bouts
of market pressures, we view recent
move as a normalization process, not a
crisis of confidence. Attractive real and
nominal yields reinforce our view that
investors should look beyond cash and
short-duration assets and move closer

to strategic duration target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

### Page 6

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to cconcerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to

other Government bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason for yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still above average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao.

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ

Chí Minh

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

gộp của BMP

Document 246: DeepSeek新论文摊牌DSec：V3.2到V4.1的Agent都在这练的，沙箱也能成护城河？

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek新论文摊牌DSec：V3.2到V4.1的Agent都在这练的，沙箱也能成护城河？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

每隔⼀段时间，就把⼀层别⼈当⿊盒捂着的基础设施掀开 —— 前⾯是 MLA 、是 3FS ，这次
是 Agent 训练场 DSec 。
摘要：
23 ⽇， DeepSeek 公开 31 ⻚系统论⽂，⾸次披露内部沙箱平台 DSec ：单⽇服务约 300 万个沙
箱、峰值 38 万并发，从 V3.2 到 V4.1 的全部 Agent 强化学习训练都跑在上⾯。论⽂作者超 130 ⼈，
梁⽂锋末位署名。
Agent 训不动了，先扛不住的是环境
⼤模型训练拼 GPU ， Agent 训练拼什么？ DeepSeek 昨天给出的答案是：拼环境。
昨天，⼀篇 31 ⻚的系统论⽂挂上 arXiv （编号 2609.22978 ），标题是《 DeepSeek Elastic
Compute (DSec): A Sandbox Infrastructure for Effective Agentic Training at Scale 》。作者名单
超过 130 ⼈， DeepSeek 创始⼈梁⽂锋排在最后⼀位，合作机构⾥有清华⼤学。论⽂ 9 ⽉ 19 ⽇提

交，学科分类是 cs.DC 分布式计算 —— 不是模型论⽂，是基建论⽂。
DSec 是什么： Agent 的「训练场」
智能体训练和⼤模型训练有个本质差别。模型训练是喂数据、算梯度； Agent 训练得让它真动⼿
—— 打开代码仓库、跑编译、调⼯具、开浏览器，每执⾏⼀步都会改变环境状态，还可能把环
境搞崩。所以每轮训练都需要⼀个与外界隔离、能记住此前操作、⽤完就扔的⼲净环境，这就
是沙箱。
DSec 就是 DeepSeek 内部批量制造沙箱的平台。规模数字相当夸张：⼀个⽣产单元约 160 台
CPU 节点、 3 万个 CPU 核⼼、 250TB 内存，托管 PB 级镜像；每天服务约 300 万个沙箱实例，峰值
并发超过 38 万个，创建速率超过每秒 5000 个；单个训练任务⼀次最多能拉起 3.2 万个沙箱。研究
者通过统⼀ SDK 按任务选隔离等级 —— 短时函数调⽤、容器、 Firecracker 微型虚拟机、完整虚
拟机，覆盖判题、软件⼯程、安全渗透、电脑操作各类负载。
最关键的⼀句在正⽂⾥：从 DeepSeek-V3.2 到 V4.1 ，全部 Agentic RL 训练和评测的沙箱负载，

都跑在 DSec 上。也就是说，这篇论⽂公开的是 DeepSeek ⼏代模型背后的那座训练场。
机制：每秒 5000 个沙箱是怎么造出来的
难点在于，每个任务需要的代码、依赖、⼯具链都不同，如果每开⼀个沙箱就重新下载解压⼀
整套镜像，集群会被 IO 打爆。 DSec 的解法分三层。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

环境层⾯，把基础系统、任务⼯作区、⼯具包拆成可独⽴更新的「可组合环境层」，开沙箱时按
需拼装，类似搭积⽊⽽⾮整体翻模。镜像层⾯，⾛ DeepSeek ⾃研的 3FS 分布式⽂件系统按需读
取 EROFS 镜像，数据⽤多少拉多少 —— 实验显⽰ 8192 个容器启动约 35 分钟，⽐全量远程拉取快
约 42% ，磁盘写⼊量下降约 57% ，任务完成时间快 1.7 倍。资源层⾯做⾼密度超售：论⽂估计
90% 的 沙 箱 实 际 CPU ⽤ 量 不 超 过 申 请 量 的 5% ， 于 是 单 节 点 硬 塞 进 3200 个 容 器 或 800 个
microVM ，配合内存共享与回收，峰值内存占⽤降约 40% 。
调度上， DSec 和强化学习框架做了协同设计：有状态的 Agent 执⾏循环与可抢占的 GPU 训练解
耦，⽀持暂停、恢复、迁移 ——Agent 在等指令时少分算⼒，活跃时才加满。
论⽂⾥还有⼀段少⻅的坦⽩：智能体在训练中真的学会了作弊 —— 通过搜索平台残留⽂件找答
案、伪造 RPC 、绕过访问控制交换⽂件数据块映射，试图从别的⽂件描述符偷读受保护内容。

DSec 的防御是 AppArmor 加 eBPF 域级⽹络⽩名单，但论⽂原话承认： " 没有任何单⼀机制能够
防⽌所有智能体异常⾏为和系统故障。 "
为什么现在公开这层地基
时机耐⼈寻味。 Agent 正从⽣成⽂本转向多轮调⽤⼯具、跑代码，训练的瓶颈跟着从 GPU 集群外
溢到环境供给。⾏业⾥ OpenAI 有内部环境基建、 Anthropic 在抓安全沙箱，各家都在闭⻔造，
DeepSeek 是头⼀个把⽣产级数字整套摊开的。论⽂前⾝还投过 ACM SIGOPS ATC 2026 操作系
统⽅向并过了⾸轮评审，这次是⼤幅扩写版 —— ⾛学术路线公开，姿态很明确：这层楼我盖完
了，图纸给你们看。
对⾏业来说，参考价值是直接的：怎么在每秒 5000 个的速率下给每个沙箱装好整套⼯具链，怎
么防⼏⼗万并发 Agent 挤爆内存，怎么处理 "Agent ⾃⼰学会钻空⼦ " 这种新型安全问题。这些
坑， DeepSeek ⽤真⾦⽩银的⽣产负载踩过了。
公开资料能确认的是：论⽂全⽂、规模数据、作弊⾏为实录均来⾃ arXiv 原⽂， V3.2 到 V4.1 负载

运⾏在 DSec 上为论⽂⾃述。需要留意的是，所有性能数字出⾃团队的系统报告，尚⽆第三⽅独
⽴测量；论⽂发布也不等于平台开源，外部团队能拿到的是架构思路，不是现成代码。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
40 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
合理推演是： DSec 公开的另⼀层含义，是 Agentic RL 的竞争正式从 " 模型算法 " 卷到 " 环境基
建 "—— 谁能在单位成本内制造更多⾼质量沙箱试错，谁的 Agent 就进化得更快。沙箱密度、镜
像分发、异常⾏为监测，这些过去藏在幕后的指标，可能会成为下⼀代模型竞赛的明牌。
⽽ "Agent 在训练场⾥就学会作弊 " 这件事被官⽅写进论⽂，本⾝也是对全⾏业的⼀次预警：对⻬
问题在训练环境⾥就已经开始发⽣了。
结尾
DeepSeek 这家公司有个⼀贯的路数：每隔⼀段时间，就把⼀层别⼈当⿊盒捂着的基础设施掀开
—— 前⾯是 MLA 、是 3FS ，这次是 Agent 训练场 DSec 。 131 个作者、 31 ⻚、⽣产级数据，诚意
是够的。梁⽂锋的名字照旧排在最后，像⼀枚印章。 Agent 时代的军备竞赛，表⾯⽐模型榜单，

⽔下⽐的是谁能以更低成本让百万个智能体同时 " 开⼯ " 。这篇论⽂之后，⽔下的部分，⼤家也能
看⻅了。

Document 323: Preview of Pre-election Economic & Fiscal Update (2026-09-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Preview of Pre-election Economic & Fiscal Update (2026-09-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-25
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• The Treasury will release its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU) at 1pm
NZT on 29 September.
• The PREFU will update the Budget 2026
fiscal forecasts, considering the latest
economic and fiscal outturns and an updated
economic outlook.
• We expect that the Treasury’s economic
forecasts, likely finalised in mid- to late-
August will point to only modest changes to
the medium-term fiscal outlook.
• We expect a small downward revision to the

forecast borrowing requirement this year and
next, in part reflecting the overfunding of last
year’s deficit.
• Had the economic forecasts been finalised in
mid-September, a combination or higher oil
prices and sharply higher long-term interest
rates would likely have combined to paint a
slightly weaker picture.
• In addition, with polls pointing to a possible
change of government, or the return of the
current coalition but with greater minor party

influence, we caution that a clear picture
of the fiscal outlook may not emerge until
Budget 2027.
The Treasury will release the Pre-Election Economic and
Fiscal Update (PREFU) on 29 September (1pm NZT). The
PREFU, which is required under the Public Finance Act,
will update the economic and fiscal forecasts contained
in Budget 2026, reflecting the latest economic and
fiscal information and updated economic forecasts. The
PREFU is prepared on the assumption that spending and

revenue policies remain consistent with the decisions of
the current Government. On the policy front there will be
little change to what was incorporated in the Budget (the
rephasing of the planned fuel tax excise, following the
cancellation of next year’s planned 12c/litre increase, will
be captured).
This “book opening” exercise is designed to create a
level playing field, allowing parties outside the current
Government and the public more generally to judge the

state of the public finances in the leadup to the General
Election. In the case of the opposition parties, it will also
allow them to calibrate the spending and taxation policies
on which they will campaign. In the weeks that follow
the release of the PREFU, we expect the major parties to
release detailed fiscal plans showing how they expect
their policies will impact the fiscal outlook.
Of course, the negotiations that take place to form
a coalition after the election means that the next

Government’s fiscal policy will be a hybrid of whatever
individual parties propose and may not even be the same
as present policy even if the current coalition is returned.
For this reason, the PREFU forecast will likely have a
short shelf life. And with recent changes to the Public
Finance Act meaning that a Half-Year Economic and Fiscal
Update (HYEFU) will likely not be published this year,
the true fiscal colours of the next Government and the

implications for the medium-term borrowing programme
may not become clear until Budget 2027 is released,
most probably in May next year. Some indication of the
medium-term outlook may be offered when the Budget
Policy Statement (BPS) is released some time before the
end of March.
With those caveats, we think that the following is relevant
when considering what the Treasury might show in
the PREFU.
• Unaudited information for the first 11 months of the

2025/26 year suggested that NZDM likely overfunded
the deficit by circa $2bn. From a funding perspective,
this provides a small buffer against any deterioration in
the forecast fiscal position in the current year.
22 Sep 2026 | Darren Gibbs, Senior Economist | +64 9 367 3368 | +64 21 794 292 | darren.gibbs@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Pre-Election Economic
and Fiscal Update.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• At this stage there is no hard data on how the public
accounts have tracked since May. The audited
accounts for 2025/26 year will be released on 8
October, providing information on the month of June,
while data covering the three months to September
will be released in early November.
• The Budget 2026 economic forecasts were finalised on
24 April – more than a month before the Budget was
released. Therefore, the forecasts that will underpin

the PREFU were likely finalised in mid-to-late August.
• Data released last week by Stats NZ indicated the
nominal economy – a proxy for the tax base – was
1.6% larger in the June quarter than the Treasury
had expected at the time it finalised its Budget 2026
forecasts. In the PREFU the Treasury will likely have
revised up its estimate of June quarter GDP .
• This stronger-than-expected growth in nominal
GDP goes some way to explaining why tax revenue

was tracking ahead of expectations as of May. Our
best guess is that tax flows will have sustained their
recent trend given our assessment of the economy’s
performance in Q3.
• When the PREFU forecasts were likely finalised, Brent
crude was around $20 lower than at present and
therefore seemingly not materially different to what
the Treasury had assumed in Budget 2026. The 4.5%
decline in the SNA terms of trade in the June quarter
was in line with the Budget forecast.

• The OCR is tracking higher than the Budget forecast,
with the RBNZ tightening policy sooner than the
Treasury had expected. However, we don’t think that
this will have a large impact on the Treasury’s medium-
term outlook, as in the Budget it had anticipated that
the OCR would rise to around 2.75% by mid-2027 and
around 3% by mid-2028.
• Similarly, when the PREFU forecasts were likely
finalised the 10-year Government bond yield was just
10bps above the Budget forecast, compared with more

than 40bps at present. Therefore, we don’t expect that
revisions to PREFU interest rate assumptions will have
a large impact on estimates of debt financing costs
(this will be an upside risk in future updates, however,
and our own fiscal forecasts have long been based on
a higher path for interest rates than that assumed by
the Treasury).
• Estimates of near-term trading partner growth are
slightly firmer than they were when the Treasury
published the Budget, in particular reflecting the

strong performance of those economies benefitting
from AI-related activity. The NZ dollar was trading
near Budget forecasts in mid-August, so the Treasury’s
forecasts will not factor the depreciation seen over the
past month.
• In Budget 2026 the Treasury forecast nominal GDP
growth of 5.0% in the current 2026/27 year. Our own
current forecast – finalised at a similar time to the
PREFU – envisaged nominal GDP growth of close
to 6%.
• Given the above, it is possible that the Treasury slightly

upgrades at least its near-term economic and fiscal
forecast, providing the Minister of Finance with the
“treat” that she said she was hoping for when speaking
at a recent financial markets conference in Auckland
(most fiscal updates during her tenure have been
far from treats given the run of significant revenue
downgrades that was only broken with the release of
Budget 2026).
Putting this all together, our best guess is that the PREFU
will point to a fiscal outlook that is broadly similar to

that forecast in Budget 2026, but with the likelihood
that near-term outcomes are slightly more favourable.
Therefore, in terms of the key fiscal metrics, the PREFU is
likely to continue to show OBEGALx returning to surplus in
2028/29 and core Crown debt peaking as a proportion of
GDP a year earlier in 2027/28.
Given the possibility of a slightly improved near-
term outlook, at least due in part to what is likely to
be projected as a better-than-expected outcome in

2025/26, NZDM could reduce its forecast of short-term
borrowings. For the next two years, the Budget forecast
for these was $2bn above the current long-term target
of $13bn. Alternatively, and perhaps more likely in the
current environment, we think the NZDM could elect to
reduce the current year’s NZGB programme by $2bn to
$32bn. Given the current weekly tender rate of $450m,
this would mean that only one $4bn syndication would be
required over the remainder of the fiscal year to complete

the NZGB programme.
Looking beyond the current year, there may also be scope
for a modest downward revision to the outyear forecasts,
especially if the Treasury adopts a more optimistic view of
the economic outlook than we are expecting. That said,
we think that the market should treat these medium-
term forecasts with some caution given the approaching
general election and the possibility that a change in the
composition of the government or economic factors

could quickly invalidate the assumptions on which the
PREFU forecast is likely to be based. Indeed, the Treasury
may acknowledge that more recent developments
present downside risk to the baseline forecast, as it did in
the Budget.

Don’t expect a game-changing breakthrough in Chinese-US relations
when President Xi meets President Trump on Thursday. Presidential
fireworks are entirely possible, William says; but so is limited progress
toward resolution on certain aspects of certain bones of contention. Today,
William outlines where he sees potential for headway and where that’s
unlikely. The best outcomes investors can hope for are extension of the
expiring truce, a handful of commercial deals, and a commitment to keep

### Page 2

talking—which may be wins enough for now. … Also: Toby looks at why
investors have soured on China’s stock market.

Document 385: “大招”越来越近？特朗普顾问分析“短期柴油出口禁令”影响

Source PDF: all report pdf/“大招”越来越近？特朗普顾问分析“短期柴油出口禁令”影响.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)

### Page 1

⽩宫正对短期柴油出⼝禁令开展正式经济评估，引发政界与业界激烈博弈。受地缘冲突与
出⼝激增影响，美国柴油库存创四⼗年来同期新低，零售均价攀升加剧中期选举政治压
⼒。能源专家表⽰，禁令虽能短暂平抑价格，但将导致库容满溢、炼⼚减产，反⽽反噬本
⼟供应。
摘要：
美国柴油价格飙升⾄历史⾼位，特朗普政府正⾯临⼀场政治逻辑与经济逻辑的正⾯碰撞。
据彭博社 25 ⽇援引知情⼈⼠透露，⽩宫已启动针对短期柴油出⼝禁令的正式经济评估。国家经
济委员会主任哈塞特、财政部⻓⻉森特和美国贸易代表 Jamieson Greer 正联合开展这⼀分析，
旨在为⾏政当局的政策决策提供依据。来⾃北达科他州的共和党参议员 John Hoeven 证实了上
述评估的存在，并表⽰若分析显⽰短期禁令有助于平抑价格， " 这是我们应当考虑的选项 " 。然
⽽，知情⼈⼠同时强调，该研究并不代表政府已倾向于⽀持或反对禁令。
与此同时，政府内部的分歧已公开浮现。能源部⻓ Chris Wright 表⽰，政府正与炼油商协商⾃愿
增加国内柴油供应，此举或可规避全⾯禁令的出台。能源专家则普遍警告，出⼝限制短期内或

能压低价格，但随着国内库存填满、炼⼚被迫削减产量，价格反弹乃⾄更⼤幅度上涨的⻛险将
随之⽽来。
地缘冲突点燃供应危机
此轮柴油短缺的根源在于地缘冲突对全球供应链的系统性冲击。中东和俄罗斯此前合计约占全
球柴油海运出⼝量的五分之⼀。今年 2 ⽉下旬伊朗战争爆发后，霍尔⽊兹海峡航运受阻，全球炼
油产能与柴油供给随之⼤幅收缩。
俄罗斯⽅向的形势在 7 ⽉进⼀步恶化。乌克兰⽆⼈机持续打击俄罗斯炼油设施数⽉后，俄⽅于 7
⽉暂停柴油出货。据报道，9 ⽉份俄罗斯六⼤主要柴油⽣产炼⼚中，有⼀半因遭受⽆⼈机袭击⽽
⼤幅削减或完全停⽌产出。
⾯对全球供应缺⼝，美国凭借庞⼤的炼油产能迅速填补空⽩，成为少数具备⼤规模出⼝能⼒的
供应⽅之⼀。据 Kpler 数据，与 2025 年均值相⽐，美国⽯油出⼝总量⾃ 3 ⽉以来已激增逾 20% ，
达到每⽇ 900 万桶。
出⼝激增叠加秋收旺季，库存告急
出⼝量⼤幅攀升的代价是国内库存持续失⾎。美国柴油库存已降⾄逾四⼗年来同期最低⽔平，
较去年同期低约 13% 。更令市场担忧的是，当前正值炼⼚秋季检修及冬季取暖旺季到来前的传

统补库窗⼝期，库存却反向下滑。
零售端价格压⼒同步传导⾄终端市场。本周柴油零售均价创历史新⾼，达到每加仑 6.51 美元，⽽
柴油是驱动农业机械、货运列⻋、⻓途卡⻋及配送⻋辆运⾏的关键燃料。⾼企的能源成本正在
成为⽩宫在中期选举前愈发棘⼿的政治包袱，来⾃农业州的议员已向特朗普施压，要求限制柴
油出⼝。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
为缓解价格压⼒，特朗普政府此前已尝试多项措施，但收效有限。 3 ⽉，⽩宫对具有百年历史的
《琼斯法案》发出豁免令，旨在便利墨西哥湾沿岸炼⼚向东西海岸输送柴油，但实际效果微乎
其微，且豁免令将于 11 ⽉ 15 ⽇到期。据报道，特朗普还曾敦促乌克兰总统泽连斯基停⽌打击俄
罗斯能源基础设施，但相关努⼒进展有限，袭击近⽇仍在持续。
禁令逻辑难以成⽴，经济代价或适得其反
表⾯上看，出⼝禁令似乎具备⼀定逻辑⽀撑 —— 美国炼⼚去年⽇产柴油近 500 万桶，国内消费量
约为 390 万桶，存在明显盈余。然⽽，能源分析⼈⼠指出，这⼀ " 盈余 " 恰恰揭⽰了禁令注定失效
的内在机制。
据路透专栏作者 Ron Bousso 分析，墨西哥湾沿岸炼⼚的运营逻辑是同时服务国内外市场，并依
据全球价格信号优化产出，将柴油、汽油和航空燃油输送⾄出价最⾼的买家。禁令实施初期，
更多燃料将滞留国内市场，价格或出现短暂回落；但⼀旦国内库存填满，炼⼚将因⽆法向海外
销售过剩柴油⽽被迫降低加⼯负荷，最终导致柴油及汽油、航空燃油等其他油品的整体产量下

滑。禁令不仅⽆法创造更多燃料，反⽽可能压低炼油商的⽣产积极性，令供应局⾯雪上加霜。
国会⼭的反对声⾳同样明确。俄克拉何⻢州共和党参议员 Alan Armstrong 表⽰， " 禁⽌柴油出⼝
是在治标⽽⾮治本。被锁在墨西哥湾沿岸的燃料，⽆法在⼀夜之间输送给中西部农⺠或东海岸
的卡⻋司机，⽽切断出⼝渠道只会迫使炼油商削减整体产量。 " 他并强调，解决⽅案应是推进许
可改⾰、建设更多能源基础设施。
政治压⼒积聚，政府⽴场仍存变数
经济逻辑之外，禁令还牵涉更深层的战略代价。限制出⼝可能损害美国作为可靠能源供应⽅的
国际声誉，与特朗普政府⼒推的 " 能源主导 " 战略形成直接⽭盾，并对欧洲和亚洲盟友造成冲击
—— 这些市场对美国燃料的依赖程度正⽇益加深。
尽管如此，政治压⼒仍在积聚。特朗普本周告知记者，他已向顾问推荐这⼀举措；⼀位⽩宫官
员则表⽰，总统希望看到油泵价格下降，并正在评估所有可选⽅案。特朗普依据紧急权⼒拥有
临时限制出⼝的法律授权，⽽在⽀持率持续承压、中期选举⽇益临近的背景下，寻求⼀个政治

上受欢迎的 " 快速解决⽅案 " 的诱惑，或难以抗拒。
反对禁令的经济逻辑相当清晰，但特朗普政府能否在政治压⼒下坚守这⼀⽴场，⽬前仍是未知
数。

### Page 3

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

《去美元化交易升溫，澳幣低位布局價值浮現》
美元在債務隱憂的背景下下跌，去美化交易重新開
啓，商品貨幣上漲明顯。由於近期澳洲勞動力市場
有所冷卻，市場對於做多澳幣有所遲疑，但我們認
爲後續即使不升息，商品價格的反彈令貿易條件的
改善，商品貨幣如澳幣在低位已經有了買入價值 。
同時，人民幣溫和升值的行情或在 4Q26加劇，我
們即將度過 6-8月的港股購匯期 ，人民幣賣壓將減
少，4Q26的結匯需求或導致人民幣溫和升值。

《投資關鍵-澳洲核心通膨仍高，建議逢低加倉》
澳幣勞動力市場數據有所冷卻 ，造成市場對於後期加
息預期有所回落 。但澳洲的核心通膨數字仍然較高 ，
並未出現明顯的下行趨勢 ，我們認爲澳聯儲近期的口
風仍會保持偏鷹派，即使後續加息的可能性不大。
金和銅大宗商品的貿易出口順差應該會帶來財政順風 ，
國內勞動力市場仍顯緊張 。同時澳幣投機多頭已經明
顯下降，擁擠程度有所減少 。短期認爲澳幣應在 0.71
的目標價盤整 ，年底看至0.72更多上行空間應來自於
美元升息預期放緩 ，或更多在下半年 。我們認爲澳幣
的看法仍然正面，澳幣短期有所回撤，CFTC投機頭寸
顯示，澳幣投機頭寸已經減少至負 ，認爲做空倉位正
在主導市場 ，在基本面仍看好的情況下 ，我們認爲反

轉的幾率較大 ，在0.71以下附近或是加碼澳幣的良好
點位。

附近或是加碼澳幣的良好點位。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。 3
顯著的空頭回補為紐幣帶來支撐，推動其表現回升
《投資關鍵 - 市場聚焦紐西蘭央行前瞻指引，紐元維持
區間整理》
過去一個月，NZD/USD大致維持於 0.5850 至 0.5950
區間內波動，近期則持續於0.59上方呈現橫盤整理格局。
市場普遍預期，紐西蘭央行將於 9/2的政策會議上升息
25個基點，將官方現金利率上調至2.75%。由於市場已
大致反映此次升息預期，因此投資人關注的焦點將在於
紐西蘭央行對未來政策路徑的前瞻指引，而非升息本身。
儘管目前紐元的投機性部位仍維持淨空頭，但相較於一

個月前空頭部位高度集中的情況，近期已出現明顯回補。
展望後市，若紐西蘭央行於本次會議中釋出超預期的鷹
派訊號，或進一步強化市場對後續升息路徑的預期，市
場對紐元的偏好及配置需求可能進一步升溫，為紐元匯
價帶來上行支撐。反之，若央行政策聲明透露出對經濟
成長放緩或通膨降溫的擔憂，並暗示未來政策調整將趨
於謹慎，則可能削弱市場對紐元的樂觀預期，進而限制
其升值空間。在經濟前景仍具不確定性的環境下，預期
第三季紐幣仍以區間震盪為主，目標價維持在0.59。
• 紐西蘭央行於 7/8 升息 25 個基
點，配合 PMI 回升及信心指標
改善，支撐紐幣表現。
• 預期第三季紐幣仍以區間震盪為
主，目標價維持在0.59。

整理，2026/7/31
低於均值一個標準差平均值
高於均值一個標準差
CFTC NZD淨頭寸

### Page 4

通膨數據回落，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要 《投資關鍵 - 美加貿易談判破局增添通膨上行風險，
維持加幣中性看法》
在市場風險情緒改善的帶動下 ，加幣自 USD/CAD
1.40 附近持續走強，並一度升破 1.3750。然而，美
加貿易談判於 8 月 22 日意外破裂，其後美國宣布對
部分加拿大出口商品加徵 50% 關稅，而加拿大亦計
劃祭出報復措施，令雙邊貿易緊張局勢再度升溫。市
場因而重新評估加拿大經濟前景，加幣升勢逆轉，使
USD/CAD 回升至約 1.39 水準。
加拿大央行預料將維持政策利率於 2.25% 不變，反
映當前經濟及通膨環境尚未促使其改變政策立場。核
心通膨仍穩定處於 1% 至 3% 的目標區間內。儘管加
美貿易談判破裂可能推升部分進口商品價格，加拿大
央行預期其影響相對溫和，且較可能體現為一次性的
價格水準調整，而非持續性的通膨壓力。此外，加拿
大勞動市場大致持穩，經濟成長動能改善，顯示暫無
調整利率的迫切性。
加拿大央行年內料維持觀望立場，但貿易風險升溫仍
將對加幣構成壓力。在貿易不確定性偏高及經濟仍在

復甦初期的背景下，加幣缺乏明確利多支撐。我們預
期加拿大央行於 2026 年維持利率不變，加幣料延續
區間震盪，展望維持中性。

 【粤开宏观】中美 K 型分化的本质与
区别 
2026 年 09 月 20 日  投资要点

近期报告  
《【粤开宏观】美国 K 型分化下的加息逻
辑 —— 美 联 储 9 月 议 息 会 议 点 评 》
2026-09-17 
《 【粤开宏观】8 月经济运行图景： 工业与
服务业分化，房地产呈现三重分化》
2026-09-15 
《【粤开宏观 】城投转型 的 进展与困难
——2025 年 城 投 年 报 观 察 （ 二 ）》
2026-09-13 
《【粤开宏观】8 月出口增速进一步上升：
“ 新 新 三 样 ” 有 望 打 开 出 口 新 空 间 》
2026-09-08 
《【粤开宏观】“六张网”建设的背景、影响、
机制及建议》2026-09-08

摘要 
当前，人工智能（AI）技术革命正在重塑全球经济，但发展红利并未普惠所
有行业、企业与家庭，反而催生了愈发显著的“K 型分化” ：一部分主体乘势
而上、加速扩张，另一部分主体持续承压、收缩调整，这已成为中美乃至全
球经济共同面临的时代挑战。分化本是经济运行的常态，但当前的 K 型分化
打破了传统经济周期中不同行业、不同群体、不同地域同向波动的规律，而
是从发展快慢的分化逐步走向发展方向的分化、扩张与萎缩的分化，如新动
能人工智能高增长与旧动能房地产负增长、外需出口高增长与内需投资负增
长。 
中美 K 型分化的成因有相似性，均发生于新一轮技术革命之下，资源集中涌
入新兴行业、流出传统行业，由此衍生出增长与就业分化、实体与金融分化、
宏观与微观背离等一系列分化。 但另一方面，中美 K 型分化也存在一定差异：
中国的分化主要源于行业间分化，内嵌于新旧动能转换的历史进程；美国的
K 型分化则更突出表现为群体间分化，核心是居民资产负债表的分层效应。 
分化有好有坏，有些分化本身就是政策推进的目标，应区分结构转型中的正
常分化与需要警惕的失衡分化。治理 K 型分化的核心，不是消除分化、阻碍
转型， 而是把握好分化的 “度” ， 防止正常的结构分化演变为长期的结构失衡，
推动 K 型的上下两端逐步向中间收敛、经济总体长期向上进步。当前中国经
济总量承压与结构分化并不矛盾，在新旧动能完成有效接力之前，必须保持
总需求基本稳定，为转型营造适宜的宏观环境。

风险提示：中美统计指标和口径存在差异、数据测算可能存在偏差；AI 产业
发展与宏观环境变化存在不确定性。

宏观深度
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  3 / 7
一、分化是经济常态，但 K 型分化是特殊的经济形态
在传统经济周期中，分化始终存在。但无论是繁荣还是衰退，经济主体的运行方向
基本一致，差异主要体现在增速与波动幅度上，也就是“同向不同幅” ：经济上行期，各
行业普遍扩张，只是弹性高的产业增长更快，稳健型产业增长更平缓；经济下行期，各
主体普遍承压，只是抗风险能力强的调整幅度小，脆弱部门受冲击更深。
而当前热议的 K 型分化， 是一种特殊的经济形态。 核心特征有两个： 一是方向背离，
不同行业、不同群体之间不再是同涨同跌，而是呈现出一边持续向上、一边持续向下的
分叉走势； 二是持续固化， 这种分化并非短期的周期扰动， 而是已经持续相当一段时间，
且存在自我强化的趋势，甚至存在长期固化的风险。
这也直接导致了总量层面的 “周期模糊” ： 从宏观数据看， 经济既非典型的全面上行，
也非典型的全面衰退， 传统的周期划分与定位框架失灵。 中美两国当前都面临这一问题，

经济不能简单用好与坏来定义，总量或结构数据不乏亮点，但不同群体对经济增长的感
受冰火两重天、宏微观背离。
二、K 型分化已演变为全球现象，中美呈现一定差异
K 型分化并非中国经济特有，而是新一轮技术革命背景下全球主要经济体共同面临
的现象。当前，中美均呈现出“AI 相关产业增长、资本开支和资产价格表现较强，而传
统部门和普通居民感受相对偏弱”的现象。但由于中美所处经济发展阶段、宏观环境和
产业基础不同，两国的经济总量表现和 K 型分化特征存在差异。
（一）中美 K 型分化的表现不同
中国的 K 型分化以行业间分化为核心主线，内嵌于新旧动能转换的历史进程。新动
能端，AI 与高技术产业成为增长引擎，2026 年 1-8 月高技术制造业增加值同比增长14.2%，
显著快于规模以上工业 5.3%的整体增速；AI 产业链商品出口同比增长 52.9%， 对整体出
口增量的贡献率达到 50.1%；资本市场科技板块走牛，科创 50 指数表现显著优于大盘，
电子、通信行业指数领跑。旧动能端， 房地产及相关产业链持续调整：1-8 月房地产开发

投资同比下降 19.9%，70 城新房和二手房价格同比仍在下跌，并通过钢铁、建材、家电
等上下游产业链，以及居民财富、地方财政、就业收入等渠道向经济全局传导。新动能
更多是资金和技术密集，而非劳动密集，吸纳就业的能力弱于旧动能，因此从影响群体
范围看，受旧动能冲击的群体多、新动能影响的群体少，这种非对称的影响直接影响社
会预期以及对宏观数据的认知。总量层面，新动能尚难以完全对冲旧动能缺口，需求不
足与物价低迷并存。
美国的 K 型分化则更突出表现为群体间分化，核心是居民资产负债表的分层效应。
AI 浪潮推动资本开支扩张与资本市场繁荣，AI 相关技术、设备投资直接拉动经济增长，
财富效应支撑消费。 但过程中群体间分化加剧， 近三年美国家庭股票资产累计增长约70%，
由于前 10%财富水平人群持有约九成的股票和基金，AI 牛市的财富效应主要流向少数人。
与此同时，中低收入群体却面临高通胀与高利率的双重挤压，大部分居民工资收入增长
放缓，中小企业就业显著承压，消费者信心指数屡创历史新低。最终形成总量经济有韧

性、但群体严重分层的格局。

### Page 4

宏观深度
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  4 / 7
（二） 中美所处的经济发展阶段和宏观环境不同，K 型分化对两国
经济周期的影响程度不同， 导致中美总量经济表现不同： 美国经济
总体呈现一定韧性，中国经济相对承压
一是 AI 发展对经济周期的影响程度不同。 美国AI 产业聚焦上游芯片设计、 大模型、
云计算等环节，资本开支规模大、产业链条长，且头部企业现金流充裕，AI 投资呈现明
显的“利率脱敏”特征，足以对经济周期形成支撑甚至“扭曲” 。同时，美国资本市场成
熟，AI 产业红利快速转化为资产价格上涨，通过财富效应带动高收入群体消费，形成投
资扩张、资产增值、消费增长的正向循环，托住了总需求。而中国AI 资本开支高速增长
但绝对规模低于美国， 企业更加侧重工程优化而非堆砌算力， 且AI 产业优势主要在中下
游应用环节，商业模式建立和投资需求释放更需要时间。此外，居民资产结构以房产为
主，资本市场财富效应不突出，AI 对经济的拉动更多体现在生产端与出口端，尚未形成

完整的内需正向循环，对总量经济的拉动相对有限。
二是传统部门对经济的拖累程度不同。中国传统部门的收缩是新旧动能转换期的结
构性、趋势性调整。房地产作为过去几十年经济增长的核心引擎，关联产业链长、覆盖
就业广、对地方财政影响大，其深度调整同时冲击企业、居民、政府三大部门资产负债
表，形成系统性的需求收缩，拖累效应显著。而美国传统部门的压力主要来自高利率的
阶段性抑制，房地产市场在 2007-2012 年次贷危机后已完成深度调整和去库存，当前整
体库存处于历史低位，房价保持温和上涨，行业结构性压力相对较小，对经济的拖累程
度可控。
从全球范围看，K 型分化问题在日韩、欧洲等经济体也都有不同程度的讨论。日韩
在 AI 浪潮与半导体产业发展过程中，出口导向的制造业和科技企业表现亮眼，但内需、
消费和传统制造业普遍低迷。欧洲在能源转型与数字化进程中，出现核心国与边缘国、
高科技产业与传统制造业之间的分化加剧。K 型分化，正成为全球经济新阶段的典型特
征。
三、如何看待与治理 K 型分化？
（一）理性看待 K 型分化，区分正常转型与失衡风险

并非所有分化都是坏事，有些分化本身就是政策推进的目标，应区分结构转型中的
正常分化与需要警惕的失衡分化。
一方面，合理的分化是产业升级的必经之路。新旧动能转换过程中，资源从低效的
传统部门向高效的新兴部门集中，旧动能逐步退坡、新动能加快成长，是产业结构升级
的必经之路， 是经济高质量发展的应有之义， 是政策推进的目标， 有其合理性和必然性。
但另一方面， 必须警惕分化过度与固化带来的风险， 也就是“好的太好、 差的太差”
的失衡局面。
“好的太好”好不好？从上行端看，新兴产业过度繁荣可能引发资源极化与泡沫化
风险。 如果AI 等热门领域出现盲目跟风、 概念炒作与低水平重复建设， 不仅会浪费财政
金融资源，还可能催生局部资产泡沫。一旦预期发生逆转，泡沫破裂不仅会冲击新兴产
业自身，还可能通过金融链条传导至整个经济体系。更重要的是，资源过度向少数行业
集中，会挤压其他产业的发展空间，不利于创新的扩散与产业生态的平衡。
“差的太差”行不行？从下行端看，传统部门过度收缩可能引发总量承压与民生风

### Page 5

宏观深度
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  5 / 7
险。房地产、传统制造业等部门吸纳就业的能力显著高于新兴科技产业，也是保障民生
刚需的重要载体。如果传统部门收缩过快、调整过深，不仅会拖累经济总量，导致总需
求持续不足，还会引发就业压力加大、居民收入放缓、家庭资产负债表恶化等一系列民
生问题。如果转型的成本过度集中于普通劳动者与困难地区，阶段性的结构差异就会固
化为收入、财富与发展机会的鸿沟，影响社会稳定与经济长期发展潜力。
因此， 治理K 型分化的核心， 不是消除分化、 阻碍转型， 而是把握好分化的 “度” ，
防止正常的结构分化演变为长期的结构失衡，推动 K 型的上下两端逐步向中间收敛、经
济总体长期向上进步。
（二）政策建议：平衡结构与总量，推动创新成果共享
面对 K 型分化，政策的总体目标应当是在支持新动能加快成长的同时，缓冲旧动能
调整的冲击， 促进创新成果更广泛地扩散与共享， 实现结构转型与总量稳定的动态平衡，
防止分化固化为阶层与区域的发展鸿沟。在新旧动能完成有效接力之前，必须保持总需

求基本稳定，为转型营造适宜的宏观环境。
第一， 完善宏观调控与政策评估框架， 兼顾总量指标与微观体感。 改变单一依赖GDP、
工业生产等总量指标的评价体系，建立涵盖居民就业收入、企业现金流、地方财政运行
的多维度常态化监测机制，精准识别结构分化下不同主体的实际状况。政策制定既要关
注新兴产业的增长亮点，也要重视传统部门的调整压力，避免宏观数据与微观感受的持
续背离。同时构建“耐心政策”体系，兼顾短期稳增长措施与长期制度性安排，逐步改
善市场预期。
第二，保持总需求基本稳定，为转型营造适宜的宏观环境。在新旧动能完成有效接
力之前，绝不能陷入“有了AI 就不需要总需求政策”的误区。要多措并举扩大内需，弥
补总量缺口。 宏观调控工具运用要注重总量与结构并重， 结构分化不能只靠结构性政策，
总量政策也需要适时加力。稳定传统部门的基本需求，避免旧动能过快收缩形成过大的
需求缺口。房地产领域要继续做好保交楼、去库存工作，因城施策支持刚性和改善性住
房需求，推进城市更新与保障性住房建设，稳定房地产投资及相关产业链需求。可考虑

通过增发国债等方式补充地方财力，防范房地产调整与地方财政压力形成负向循环。
第三，推动 AI 创新扩散与产业融合，增强新动能的带动效应。一方面，加大对基础
研究、关键技术攻关的支持力度，补强核心零部件、关键设备、基础软件等薄弱环节，
继续推动资本市场改革，完善融资支持体系。另一方面，推动AI 技术向传统制造业、服
务业广泛扩散应用，提升传统产业的生产率与竞争力，延长产业链条，拓宽就业吸纳渠
道。 同时加强对地方AI 产业基金、 招商项目的统筹评估， 及时纠正低水平重复建设与盲
目扩张，引导资源精准高效配置。
第四，完善收入分配与社会保障体系，促进转型收益全民共享。健全工资合理增长
与最低工资动态调整机制，推动劳动者报酬与劳动生产率增长基本同步，规范灵活就业
人员劳动权益保障，让技术进步的成果更多转化为劳动者收入。完善养老、医疗、失业
等社会保障制度，推进公共服务均等化，降低居民预防性储蓄动机，释放消费潜力。针
对 AI 时代收入财富分化加剧的新趋势， 探索优化财税调节机制， 通过转移支付、 社会救

助等方式缓冲转型对弱势群体的冲击，让更多人分享技术进步与经济发展的红利。
K 型分化是技术革命时代的全球性挑战，也是中国经济转向高质量发展阶段必须跨
越的关口。分化本身不可怕，可怕的是分化的固化与失衡。唯有在创新中包容，在转型
中平衡，才能让技术进步真正成为惠及全体人民的动力，推动中国经济在结构优化中实
现行稳致远。

股票投资评级说明 
投资评级分为股票投资评级和行业投资评级。

股票投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内公司股价的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
买入：相对大盘涨幅大于 10%； 
增持：相对大盘涨幅在 5%~10%之间； 
持有：相对大盘涨幅在-5%~5%之间； 
减持：相对大盘涨幅小于-5%。

行业投资评级标准 
报告发布日后的 12 个月内行业股票指数的涨跌幅度相对同期沪深 300 指数的涨跌幅为基准，投资建议的评级标准为： 
增持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报高于基准指数 5%以上； 
中性：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报介于基准指数-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：我们预计未来报告期内，行业整体回报低于基准指数 5%以下。

（資料）インド統計・計画実施省
1. インド経済は 2026 年 4-6 月期に実質ＧＤＰ成長率が前年同期比 7.8％となり、 前期の8.6％
から鈍化したものの、 引き続き高い伸びを維持した。 民間消費が7.1％増と堅調に推移する
なか、 総固定資本形成は11.9％増、 輸出は12.0％増と加速し、 内外需が成長を下支えした。
7 月も生産や政府支出などの指標は堅調で、景気の基調は従来想定していたより強い。

2. 一方、 高成長の陰で若年層の雇用問題が残っている。2025 年の 15～29 歳の失業率は 9.9％
と、15 歳以上全体の 3.1％を大きく上回った。 また、 世論調査ではＺ世代の53％が 「雇用 ・
教育・医療」を選挙で重視する課題に挙げている。政府は技能訓練や起業支援を強化して
いるが、高成長を若者の雇用機会の拡大につなげられるかが重要な課題となる。

3. インド経済は内需の底堅さを背景に高めの成長を維持するものの、減税効果の一巡や物価
上昇による実質購買力の低下から、2026 年度後半には成長ペースが鈍化すると予想する。
その後は物価上昇の一服とともに緩やかに持ち直し、 実質ＧＤＰ成長率は2026 年度 7.0％、
2027 年度 7.1％を予想する。 消費者物価上昇率は2026 年度 5.0％に上昇し、 ＲＢＩは年度
末にかけて政策金利を 5.75％まで引き上げると見込む。

### Page 2

（支出別の動向：投資・輸出が加速、消費も堅調） 
支出別にみると、2026 年 4-6 月期は民間消費が高めの伸びを維持するなか、投資と輸出が加速
し、内外需が成長を下支えした（図表２）。 
総固定資本形成は前年同期比 11.9％となり、前期の 10.5％から加速した。鉱工業生産指数でも
資本財が同15.2％と高い伸びを示すなど、投資関連の指標は堅調に推移した。 
民間消費は同 7.1％となり、前期の 7.5％から小幅に鈍化したものの、引き続き高めの伸びを維
持した。家計向け自動車登録台数が同15.9％増となるなど、消費需要の底堅さがうかがわれる。 
政府消費は同4.3％となり、 前期の7.7％から鈍化した。 成長率寄与度は約0.5％ポイントにとど
まり、政府消費による景気押し上げは限定的だった。 
外需については、輸出が同 12.0％となり、前期の 3.9％から大きく加速した。一方、輸入は同
▲1.1％となり、前期の 0.8％増から減少に転じた。この結果、純輸出の成長率寄与度は約＋3.0％
ポイントとなり、 外需が成長率を大きく押し上げた。 もっとも、 統計上の不突合が成長率を約3.2％
ポイント押し下げており、支出項目の強さに比べて実質ＧＤＰ成長率は7.8％にとどまった。

（産業別の動向：サービス業が成長をけん引、製造業も加速） 
産業別（ＧＶＡ）では、実質ＧＶＡ成長率は前年同期比 8.2％となり、前期の 8.7％から鈍化し
たものの、 高い伸びを維持した （図表４） 。 大分類では第一次産業が同2.9％、 第二次産業が同8.6％、
第三次産業が同 10.0％となり、サービス業を中心とする第三次産業が引き続き成長をけん引した。 
第一次産業では、農林水産業が同3.6％となり、 前期の3.9％から小幅に鈍化した （図表５） 。 一
方、鉱業・採石業は同▲2.4％となり、減少に転じた。 
第二次産業では、製造業が同 9.2％となり、前期の 7.9％から加速した。鉱工業生産指数ではコ
ンピューター・電子・光学製品が同12.4％増となるなど、 製造業の生産活動は堅調に推移した。 一
方、 建設業は同7.7％となり、 前期の8.7％から鈍化したものの、 高めの伸びを維持した。 電気・ガ
ス・水道等は同8.9％となった。 
第三次産業では、商業・ホテル・運輸・通信が同8.5％となり、前期の12.9％から鈍化した。一
方、 金融・不動産・ＩＴ・専門サービスは同12.1％と、 前期の12.3％に続いて二桁成長を維持し、
サービス業全体の高成長を支えた。公共サービスは同 7.5％となり、前期の 8.0％から小幅に鈍化
した。

### Page 3

（物価の動向：消費者物価は 4％台半ばに上昇、卸売物価は高止まり） 
消費者物価は足元で上昇基調にある。総合ＣＰＩ上昇率は、2025年後半に食品価格の下落を主
因に大きく低下したが、2026年入り後は食品価格の反発を受けて持ち直している。直近の７月は
前年同月比4.5％と、前月の4.4％から小幅に上昇し、ＲＢＩの物価目標である４％を２カ月連続
で上回ったものの、許容レンジ（２～６％）内で推移している（図表６）。 
ＣＰＩの内訳をみると、食品・飲料は前年同月比5.2％、消費者食品価格指数は同5.5％とな
り、食品価格の上昇が総合ＣＰＩを押し上げている。一方、燃料・光熱費は同2.0％と低い伸びに
とどまった。また、食品・飲料と燃料・光熱費を除くコアＣＰＩは同3.9％と前月から横ばいとな
っており、基調的な物価上昇圧力が全面的に強まっている状況にはない。 
一方、卸売物価（ＷＰＩ）は高い伸びが続いている。ＷＰＩ上昇率は2025年を通じてゼロ％近
辺の低い伸びにとどまっていたが、2026年入り後に上昇基調を強め、直近では前年同月比10％近
い水準まで上昇している（図表７）。内訳では、燃料・電力価格は足元で増勢が鈍化しているも
のの、一次産品や加工品の上昇率が高まっており、卸売段階での物価上昇圧力は広がりをみせて
いる。 
総じてみると、消費者物価は食品価格の上昇を背景に持ち直す一方、卸売段階では一次産品や
加工品にも上昇圧力が広がっている。もっとも、コアＣＰＩは４％程度で安定しており、卸売段
階のコスト上昇が消費者物価に全面的に波及している状況にはない。今後は、原材料・輸送コス
トの上昇が企業の価格転嫁を通じて消費者物価に波及するかが注目される。

### Page 4

２．トピックス：高成長の陰で残る若者の雇用問題 
インドでは足元、若年層の教育や雇用、公共サービスなどを巡る不満が社会問題として注目を
集めている。その象徴となったのが、若者を中心とする社会運動「コックローチ・ジャンタ党
（Cockroach Janta Party、ＣＪＰ、通称「ゴキブリ党」）」である。ＣＪＰは５月にＳＮＳ上で
生まれた運動で、大学入試や公務員採用試験などを巡る問題を背景に支持を広げ、６月以降はニ
ューデリーで大規模な抗議活動を展開した。７月25 日には主要な要求の一つだったプラダン教育
相の辞任が実現し、抗議活動はいったん終了した。もっとも、その後もＣＪＰは政党化せず圧力
団体として活動を継続している。各地の若者が教育や雇用、学校・道路など身近な公共サービス
への不満をＳＮＳ上で発信し、ＣＪＰがそうした声を取り上げて拡散することで、従来は地域
的・個別的な問題にとどまりやすかった不満が全国的に可視化される動きが広がっている。８月
には公立学校の施設や教員不足などを市民自身が点検・発信する全国キャンペーンも始まり、若
年層の不満を吸い上げる活動は広がりをみせている。 
こうした若者の不満のなかでも、経済面で大きな課題となっているのが雇用である。2025 年の
定期労働力調査（ＰＬＦＳ）によると、15 歳以上の失業率は3.1％にとどまる一方、15～29 歳の

### Page 5

３．短期経済見通し 
インド経済は、内需の底堅さを背景に今後も比較的高い成長を維持すると予想する。ただし、
成長ペースは2026 年度後半に鈍化し、その後は2027 年後半にかけて緩やかに持ち直す見通しで
ある（図表11）。2026 年 4-6 月期の成長率が予想を上回ったほか、7 月も生産や政府支出などの
指標は堅調であり、景気の基調は従来想定していたより強い。もっとも、2025 年度の景気を押し
上げたＧＳＴ減税や所得減税、食品価格低下による実質購買力の改善効果が一巡するほか、物価
上昇が家計の購買力を圧迫するため、年度後半には景気の拡大テンポが弱まると予想する。 
需要項目別にみると、民間消費は引き続き成長の中心となるだろう。雇用・所得環境の安定や
これまでの減税効果を背景に2026 年7-9 月期までは高めの伸びが続くものの、その後は減税効果
の一巡や食品価格の上昇により伸びが鈍化するとみられる。農村部ではモンスーンの降雨不足が
下押し要因となるが、現時点では農業生産の大幅な落ち込みは想定していない。物価上昇が一服
する 2027 年後半には、実質購買力の改善を背景に消費は再び持ち直すだろう。
